# Hoot Hollow credits

Models, textures, artworks, photos and sounds by their authors, used under their licences (CC0, public domain, CC BY). Thank you.


---

# Credits: the game's typeface (builder font6)

- **Alagard** - Hewett Tsoi (Pix3M), https://www.dafont.com/alagard.font, "Give credit if used."
  A 16 px pixel font, supplied by the project owner. It is now the game's one typeface: every screen, button,
  sign, tag, toast, the book, the globe, the shop's signs and slate, the interview's forms, speech, name plate,
  certificates and props, the house signs and moving-box labels. Shipped unmodified as
  `src/assets/fonts/alagard.ttf`.

Glyphs Alagard does not have (typographic quotes and dashes, the middle dot, the ellipsis, accents) are turned into
their plain ASCII twins before they are drawn (`src/ui/font.ts`). Anything else falls back to the TBME Goofy pixel
face (made from Chewy by Font Diner, Apache 2.0, see `src/assets/fonts/FONTS_LICENSE.txt`). The pretend phone's
tiny system text (app labels, notifications) keeps m5x7 by Daniel Linssen (managore.itch.io), credited in
`intro4.md`. The m6x11plus and Galmuri11 faces are no longer used and were removed.


---

# furnA credits

Furniture models in `public/models/items/` for furnA's ids (`src/assets/home.ts` and the indoor pieces of
`src/assets/furniture.ts`).

## Borrowed models

Taken from the owner's games BUSHED and Gacha Garage (which keep their own upstream credits, repeated here) or
converted from Poly Haven with lowpix, then restyled for Hoot Hollow by `art/lowpix/items/_furnA/borrow.py`:
calm re-grade, k-means pixel palette (BUSHED's `tools/forest/pix.py`, copied as `_furnA/pix.py`), lit matte
material, fitted to the item's size and composed with other parts.

## Real artworks on the walls (public domain, Art Institute of Chicago)

Pixel-graded (calm grade, k-means palette) onto the canvas card of the item; source copies (<= 256 px) in
`art/lowpix/items/_furnA/art/`. Image data via the Art Institute of Chicago API (CC0 metadata, public-domain images).

| Item | Artwork | Artist | Date | Source |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| `painting` | Nocturne: Blue and Gold—Southampton Water | James McNeill Whistler (American, 1834–1903) | 1872 | [artic.edu/artworks/56905](https://www.artic.edu/artworks/56905) |
| `starmap` | Celestial Map of the Northern Sky with the Twelve Zodiac Signs | Albrecht Dürer | 1515 | [artic.edu/artworks/106550](https://www.artic.edu/artworks/106550) |

## 2D pixel-art leaves (borrowed from BUSHED)

The foliage of furnA's plants is made of 2D pixel-art leaf cards cut from BUSHED's vegetation atlas
(`public/assets/textures/veg/veg_atlas.png`, crops in `art/lowpix/items/_furnA/leaves/`), which BUSHED made from
Poly Haven CC0 sources: sword fern fronds and bracken from [Fern 02](https://polyhaven.com/a/fern_02) (Rob Tuytel,
Rico Cilliers); juniper sprigs from [Fir Tree 01](https://polyhaven.com/a/fir_tree_01) twig maps (Rob Tuytel, Rico
Cilliers); grass blades procedural (BUSHED). Used in `fernpot`, `bonsai`, `nestbed`, `mushpot`, `mushroomlamp` and
the flowers of `planter`.

## Made for Hoot Hollow (lowpix specs, CC0)

Every other furnA item (`art/lowpix/items/<id>.json`, generated by `art/lowpix/items/_furnA/items_*.py`) was
modelled and hand-painted from scratch with lowpix, as were the parts merged into borrowed items (candle
flames, the planter's flowers). Photo detail in their textures comes from CC0 Poly Haven textures
(`art/lowpix/items/_furnA/photos/`: oak_veneer_01, wood_table_001, rough_linen, hessian_230, velour_velvet,
knitted_fleece, brown_leather, clay_floor_001, metal_plate_02, jersey_melange, bark_brown_02, rock_face_03,
forest_leaves_02).

Models in `public/models/items/` for the item ids in `src/assets/decor/{beds,tables,lights,green}.ts`.

## Borrowed models

Each was taken from Gacha Garage's world props (`public/models/lowpix/world/<file>`, the user's own game, where it was
made low-poly with a hand-painted 512 px atlas from the source below), then restyled for Hoot Hollow by
`art/lowpix/items/_furnb/borrow.py` (recipe in `borrowed.py`): texture re-graded to the calm palette (less saturation,
slate shadows, lamp-gold highlights, per-item recolours), lit matte material, fitted to the item's footprint. Glowing
bulbs and flames on the two lamps are our own lowpix parts (`art/lowpix/items/_extra/`).

## Our own models

Every other id (monstera, bunk bed, canopy bed, hammock bed, basket nest, beanbag, toadstool seat, vanity, workbench, painted
dresser, library bookcase, armoire, apothecary, old-oak coffee table, fairy lights, candelabra, toadstool lamp, firefly
jar, cactus trio, trailing ivy, maple bonsai, window box, terrarium, fiddle-leaf fig and the five rugs) is modelled from
scratch as a lowpix spec (`art/lowpix/items/<id>.json`, generated by `art/lowpix/items/_furnb/*.py`), with
hand-painted textures. Every leafy plant (monstera, fiddle-leaf fig, trailing ivy, maple bonsai, geranium
window box, terrarium moss and ferns, the moss on the basket nest, the shelf plants) is built from 2D pixel-art leaf
cards that we hand-pixelled in code (`_furnb/foliage.py`). Some wood, bark, stone and leaf surfaces mix in relief from CC0 Poly Haven photo textures that
ship with lowpix (`assets/photos/`: bark_brown_02, rock_face_03, forest_leaves_02; https://polyhaven.com, CC0).

`art/lowpix/items/_furnc/build.py`.

hand-painted atlas from the source below), then restyled for Hoot Hollow by `art/lowpix/items/_furnc/compose.py`

lamp-gold highlights, hue bands mapped onto palette ramps, the typewriter's black enamel repainted sage), lit matte
material, pieces re-laid (the tea set) and fitted to the item's footprint.

## Wall art: real public-domain artworks

Every picture shown by a wall-art item is a real artwork from the Art Institute of Chicago's open collection
(public-domain images, CC0 data, fetched from `api.artic.edu` / `www.artic.edu/iiif`). The images are kept small in
`art/lowpix/items/_furnc/artworks/` (≤ 256 px JPEG) and pixel-graded onto each item's texel grid by `Spec.art_card` in
`lib.py`: cropped to the frame, resampled, slightly desaturated and warmed toward the calm palette, quantised to
44-48 colours. Nothing in them is drawn by us.

| item id | where | artwork | artist | date | source |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| `photo_frames` | big oak frame | Stacks of Wheat (End of Summer) | Claude Monet | 1890–91 | https://www.artic.edu/artworks/64818 |
| `photo_frames` | gilt oval (upper part of the print) | Cuckoo, pine branch, and full moon | Utagawa Hiroshige | c. 1843/47 | https://www.artic.edu/artworks/47547 |
| `photo_frames` | small square frame | Roses in a Bowl | Henri Fantin-Latour | 1881 | https://www.artic.edu/artworks/20534 |
| `photo_frames` | little round frame | Apples and Grapes | Claude Monet | 1880 | https://www.artic.edu/artworks/16549 |
| `owl_tapestry` | woven into the hanging | Owl on pine branch | Utagawa Hiroshige | 1830s | https://www.artic.edu/artworks/47662 |
| `hollow_map` | the whole scroll | Sampler (Map) | England (unknown maker) | c. 1800 | https://www.artic.edu/artworks/15132 |

## Foliage

Every leafy thing of mine is built from 2D hand-pixelled leaf cards (alpha-cut quads, many per plant), drawn
procedurally needle by needle and leaf by leaf in `art/lowpix/items/_furnc/foliage.py` (fir sprigs, leaf clusters,
single leaves): the Frostlight fir, wreath, stocking garland, poinsettia, the firs on the gingerbread cottage, in the
snow globe and by the toy train, the snow globe's oak crown and the bird feeder's ivy. Our own work, CC0.

## Photo textures (material relief under the painted ramps)

The lowpix-modelled items photo-bash real material relief (lowpix `photo` op, `detail` mode: the colours stay on our
palette ramps). Sources in `art/lowpix/items/_furnc/photos/` were copied from BUSHED's pixel-art texture set
(`public/assets/textures/`, made by BUSHED's `tools/make_textures.py` from Poly Haven scans); three more ship with
lowpix (`assets/photos/`).

| folder | BUSHED texture | Poly Haven scan | author | licence |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| `photos/wood` | `building/floor_boards` | https://polyhaven.com/a/wood_floor_worn | Dimitrios Savva | CC0 1.0 |
| `photos/planks` | `building/rough_planks` | https://polyhaven.com/a/weathered_planks | Dario Barresi, Dimitrios Savva | CC0 1.0 |
| `photos/stone` | `building/fieldstone` | https://polyhaven.com/a/stone_wall | Dario Barresi, Charlotte Baglioni | CC0 1.0 |
| `photos/granite` | `terrain/granite` | https://polyhaven.com/a/rock_08 | Poly Haven | CC0 1.0 |
| `photos/moss` | `terrain/moss` | https://polyhaven.com/a/sparse_grass | Poly Haven | CC0 1.0 |
| `photos/bark` | `trees/bark_pine` | Poly Haven bark scan (see BUSHED `CREDITS.md`) | Poly Haven | CC0 1.0 |
| lowpix `assets/photos/forest_leaves_02`, `rusty_metal_02`, `brown_leather` | (lowpix) | https://polyhaven.com | Poly Haven | CC0 1.0 |

## Our own models

Everything else (the Frostlight and Starfall prizes, the fun items, the wall pieces, the floor globe, record player,
ship in a bottle and the rest of the trinkets) is modelled from scratch as lowpix specs written by
`art/lowpix/items/_furnc/{trinkets,fun,frost,starfall}.py` (`art/lowpix/items/<id>.json`): low-poly lofts, turned
lathes and chamfered boxes with hand-painted texture layers, painted pixel-art cards (dials, maps, tapestry, rugs,
branches, leaves) and animation clips. CC0.

CC BY 4.0 licence text: https://creativecommons.org/licenses/by/4.0/ . CC0 1.0: https://creativecommons.org/publicdomain/zero/1.0/

# Credits: the Earth and the places (globe, round 4)

Everything listed here is public domain, CC0 or CC BY. The globe stage, its shaders, the place view and the
tools in `src/world/globe/tools/` were made for Hoot Hollow.

## The Earth (`public/models/globe/`, built by `src/world/globe/tools/earth_textures.py` and `borders.py`)

- **Day map**: NASA Blue Marble Next Generation (land) and Blue Marble with shaded bathymetry (seas), NASA Earth
  Observatory / NASA Goddard (Reto Stöckli), public domain, fetched from NASA GIBS
  (https://gibs.earthdata.nasa.gov, layers `BlueMarble_NextGeneration`, `BlueMarble_ShadedRelief_Bathymetry`).
- **Land / water mask**: NASA MODIS land-water mask 2015 (GIBS `MODIS_Terra_L3_Land_Water_Mask`), public domain.
- **City lights**: NASA Earth Observatory / NOAA NGDC, VIIRS city lights 2012 (Black Marble), public domain
  (GIBS `VIIRS_CityLights_2012`).
- **Relief (bump and sea mask)**: NOAA NCEI ETOPO1 global relief, ice surface (Amante and Eakins 2009), public
  domain, via NOAA CoastWatch ERDDAP (https://coastwatch.pfeg.noaa.gov/erddap/griddap/etopo180.html).
- **Clouds**: Solar System Scope, "8k Earth Clouds" (https://www.solarsystemscope.com/textures/), based on NASA
  imagery, CC BY 4.0 (https://creativecommons.org/licenses/by/4.0/). Shrunk to 4096 x 2048 and levelled.
- **The Milky Way**: ESO / S. Brunier, "The Milky Way panorama" (https://www.eso.org/public/images/eso0932a/),
  CC BY 4.0. Shrunk to 2048 x 1024.
- **Borders and country outlines**: Natural Earth 1:50m admin-0 boundary lines and map units, v5.1.2
  (https://www.naturalearthdata.com), public domain, via https://github.com/nvkelso/natural-earth-vector.
- **Close-ups of each place** (the satellite tile in each place's sheet): NASA Blue Marble Next Generation and its
  bathymetry version with the MODIS land-water mask, from NASA GIBS, public domain.

## Photographs of the places (`public/img/places/<place>.webp`, `places.webp`)

Three photographs per place, found through Openverse (https://openverse.org, licence filter CC0 / public domain /
CC BY) and downloaded from Flickr at 1024 px; cropped to 3:2 (`src/world/globe/tools/place_photos.py`, choices in
`photo_picks.py`). Shown in the place view with their captions and an in-app credit line. CC BY 2.0:
https://creativecommons.org/licenses/by/2.0/ (the photos were cropped and resized). CC0:
https://creativecommons.org/publicdomain/zero/1.0/.

# Credits: Pip's hollow oak, round 4 (house, rooms, furniture pass)

The oak is now built in code (`src/world/house/oak.ts`, `geo.ts`, `kit.ts`, `crown.ts`, `locks.ts`, `boxes.ts`); its
crown uses the forest's shared leaf-card canopy and leaf atlas (`src/world/foliage.ts`, credited by the world
builder). Its textures are crisp, palette-limited pixel-art versions of CC0 scans, made by `art/lowpix/house/tex.py`
from the vendored copies in `art/lowpix/house/photos/`, plus generated pixel art (moss cushions; the ivy, fern and
sprig leaf cards; the painted signs, plaques, labels and the dusty window, drawn at runtime with our own 5 x 7 pixel
font). The moving boxes, their cardboard, tape and labels are drawn in code.

`furnB.md` and `furnC.md`.

# Credits: the loading clock and Hollow & Co.'s office (builder intro4)

The loading clock (`index.html` boot block, `public/models/interview/clock.png`) is painted in code by
`src/onboard/tools/bootart.mjs`. The interview's form layer (paper, leather folio, brass clip, ink boxes and ticks,
rubber-stamp impressions, sticky note, acorns) is painted in code by `src/onboard/panelart.ts`. The office (walls,
parquet, rug, coffered ceiling, desk, bookcases and books, credenza, filing cabinets, banker's lamp, nameplate,
carafe, fountain pen, rubber stamp and ink pad, client folder, sconces, fig trees, the bracket clock) is modelled
and textured in code for Hoot Hollow (`src/onboard/{furnish,desk,mats,tex,wallclock}.ts`).

## Borrowed models

Packed by `src/onboard/tools/packglb.mjs` into `public/models/interview/cards.glb` (answer objects in the interview:
the day bed, the sheep, the alarm clock, the pocket watch, the nightstand) and `office.glb` (props in the office).
Models marked *lowpix* were converted for Hoot Hollow with the lowpix tool (pixel-HD style: decimated, one painted
colour atlas, WebP); the others were already converted by the project named in the Via column (BUSHED and Gacha
Garage are the owner's other games, which ship these models with the same credits). All were rescaled, re-encoded
and packed for Hoot Hollow. (The interview's other answer objects are modelled and painted in code: see intro5.md.)

Everything this round added is made in code for Hoot Hollow; nothing new was borrowed.

- **Answer objects** (`src/onboard/props.ts`): the espresso cup and saucer with its spoon, the stoneware coffee mug,
  the rose-painted tea cup with its tea-bag tag, the "Fizz" cola can and the "ZAP!" energy can (made-up brands),
  the cocoa mug with whipped cream and marshmallows, the water glass with ice and lemon, nine cassette tapes with
  hand-written labels and little cover pictures, the "Hollow Tone" portable cassette recorder (made-up brand), the
  pillows and their pillowcases, the pillar candle on its brass chamberstick, the broadcast microphone (and its
  knitted nightcap), the brass sun, moon and sun-and-moon ornaments, a generic smartphone, the phone's little
  wooden bed, the sheep stile and the stack of bedside books with reading glasses. Turned, extruded and swept
  geometry with hand-painted pixel textures (brushwork, hue jitter, worn edges, painted labels), painted at runtime.
- **The floating fountain pen** (`src/onboard/pen.ts`): nib, feed, section, marbled barrel, posted cap, clip and
  finial, modelled and painted in code.
- **The pretend phone** (`src/ui/phone/phone.tsx`): the body, the moonlit wallpaper, the made-up apps and their
  icons (Natter, Loopreel, Scrollie, Glimmer, Questy, Mailbox, Shoppa, Newsy), the notifications, padlocks and
  the shield, painted pixel by pixel in code. No real brand, logo or app is shown.

The sheep, day bed, alarm clock, pocket watch and nightstand in the answers are the borrowed models already
credited in `intro4.md` (repacked into `public/models/interview/cards.glb`). The phone's text uses m5x7 by Daniel
Linssen and Galmuri11 (Lee Minseo, SIL Open Font License), credited there too.

# Credits: the toy owls and their outfits (builder owl3)

## Toy library

`art/blender/lib/` (tk.py, facet.py, paint.py, face.py, fig.py, rig.py, parts.py, kit.py, _bz.py) is the owner's own
Blender toy library from **Gacha Garage** (`art/blender/toys/lib`, `sabaku/_bz.py`), copied with its comments; only its
paths were adapted. The owls, the Queen and the remade outfit pieces (`art/blender/owls/owl.py`, `outfits.py`) are
modelled and hand-painted with it for Hoot Hollow.

## Photo-bash fields (`art/blender/lib/photos/*.npz`, from Gacha Garage's `lib/photos/make.py`)

The painter lays a little real-surface detail under its brush strokes (feathers on every owl, fur on the Meowl,
pebbled skin on the gingerbread and snowman owls, scales on bare legs):

  (Open Images ID 2343212ab72aafb2).

  (licence as listed on Commons; credited in Gacha Garage's CREDITS.md "Toy textures").

# Credits: the v4 owls and their refinished outfits (builder owl4)

## The owls (`public/models/owls/<id>.glb`, `<id>.face.webp`, `<id>.full.webp`)

Modelled and painted for Hoot Hollow from scratch by `art/blender/owls/owl.py` (parametric surfaces: body, head with a
dished facial disc and ruff, folded wings with a spread morph target, tail, feathered legs, toes and talons, bill, ear
tufts) and `art/blender/owls/plume.py` (every feather laid as its own shingled stroke; species markings as stroke
layers; relief normal map; ray-traced cavity using Blender's BVH from the owner's existing `gg-bpy` install). No
photographs, scans or third-party models are used. Plumage and proportions follow field-guide descriptions of the real
birds (Tawny Owl, Barn Owl, Snowy Owl, Burrowing Owl, Northern Saw-whet Owl, Great Horned Owl, Great Grey Owl,
Eurasian Eagle-Owl); no field-guide artwork was copied.

The Queen's model and stills (`queen.*`) are untouched (owl3).

The downy fluff is drawn at runtime by `src/owl/fur.ts` (instanced shells; a strand field generated in code).

## Outfits (`public/models/outfits/*.glb`)

All 39 pieces were remodelled from scratch for Hoot Hollow by `art/blender/owls/garments.py` (parametric garments
fitted to the v4 tawny, painted procedurally: knit, felt, velvet with embroidery, satin, cotton prints, fur trims,
engraved metal, gems, glass, leather, wood, petals and leaves). They no longer use any owl3 or Gacha Garage geometry,
so the CC BY attributions in `docs/credits/owl3.md` now cover only the history of those files. No new third-party
assets.


---

# Credits: Queen Neve's boutique (shop builder)

Every model below was borrowed and restyled for Hoot Hollow: textures reduced to a single matte base colour
(normal, roughness and AO maps dropped), base colour downsized to 512 px or less, gently desaturated and tinted
towards the calm "Moss & Moonlight" palette, mesh welded and simplified towards a triangle budget, WebP + meshopt
compressed (`gltf-transform`, script kept in the builder's scratchpad: `pack.mjs`). Files live in
`public/models/shop/`. Upstream attributions are kept as the source projects list them.

## Made for the game

- The potted fir, the monstera and the trailing pothos are built from 2D pixel-art leaf cards painted in code
  (`src/world/stages/shopplants.ts`), with lathe pots.
- The view through the bay window is the live game world, rendered by the app's `Scene.renderView()`.

## Also borrowed

- The stage's picture treatment (low internal resolution, grade, split toning, ordered dither + quantise, grain)
  follows BUSHED's `src/gfx/renderer.ts` (the owner's game), simplified.
- The realistic paper surface in the shop UI follows BUSHED's `src/ui/paper.ts` (mottling, fibres, light
  quantisation).

# Credits: Barnaby's Lodge Store (shop builder, round 4)

## Made for the game

- **Barnaby Birchbark**, the beaver shopkeeper (`public/models/shop/beaver.glb`): modelled, rigged, painted and
  animated in code for Hoot Hollow (`art/shop/beaver.py` + `art/shop/paintkit.py`, Blender bpy, headless). The coat,
  muzzle, paws, tail scales and the canvas apron are painted per texel in numpy (no photo sources).
- The lodge itself (peeled-log walls, the stick ceiling, the counter, the deal stumps with their roots, the Lucky
  Barrel's cradle and crank, acorn sacks and heaps, the moon gems, the turned brass scales and their chains, the wrapped
  gift parcel and its bow, the chalk slate, the cloth banners, the carved signs, the rag rug, the herb bundles and the
  wood shavings) is built in code in `src/world/stages/lodgekit.ts` and `src/world/stages/lodge.ts`, dressed in the
  textures below and in pixel canvases painted in code. The mantel clock's real-time face and hands are drawn in code.
- The burlap weave (`public/models/shop/tex/burlap.webp`) is painted in code (`art/shop/textures.py`).
- The potted fir by the window is built from 2D pixel-art leaf cards (`src/world/stages/shopplants.ts`, round 3).

The 9-slice frames, sprites and fonts come from the owner's game The Bear Must Eat (as before); this round

# Credits: the world (builder world3)

The woods, the ground and the look of the renderer are borrowed from **BUSHED**, the owner's lumberjack game
(`lpx-bushed/games/bushed`), and restyled for Hoot Hollow's calm "Moss & Moonlight" palette (textures
desaturated and nudged towards sage, re-encoded as WebP; tints per season in `src/world/flora.ts`).
BUSHED's own sources are **CC0 1.0** scans from [Poly Haven](https://polyhaven.com), re-made by BUSHED's
`tools/forest/*.py` as high-resolution pixel art; its models were converted with lowpix.

## Code (adapted)

| Hoot Hollow | From BUSHED | Notes |
|---|---|---|
| `src/world/bushed/build.ts`, `geom.ts`, `species.ts`, `rng.ts`, `noise.ts` | `src/trees/build.ts`, `src/trees/geom.ts`, `src/trees/species.ts`, `src/core/rng.ts`, `src/core/noise.ts` | procedural trees; an `oak` species added (broadleaf path, birch leaves, spruce bark) |
| `src/world/bushed/protos.ts`, `atlas.ts` | `src/world/veg/protos.ts`, `src/world/veg/atlas.ts` | ground-cover prototypes (ferns, shrubs, cattails, lily pads, mushrooms, moss, cones) |
| `src/world/bushed/materials.ts`, `textures.ts` | `src/trees/materials.ts`, `src/trees/textures.ts`, `src/gfx/tex.ts` | wind / translucency / moss patches re-wired to our wind and sky |
| `src/world/post.ts` | `src/gfx/renderer.ts` | low internal resolution, bloom, filmic grade, split toning, vignette, grain, ordered dither, nearest upscale (atmosphere, shafts, lake and motion blur passes left out for phones) |

# Audio credits

Sound effects, ambience loops and music shipped in `public/sfx/` (9 MP3 files: 6 audio sprites holding 267 short clips and 3 file packs holding 22 loops, music tracks and alarms; 166 sound ids, 14.17 MB). The first 88 files were checked on **2026-11-10**; 35 files (28 new sound ids, 6.94 MB: alarms, breathing cues, sleep soundscapes, music loops and physical-UI foley) were added and checked on **2026-11-20**.

Every file is an edit (cut, fade, gain, EQ, mono/stereo fold, loop crossfade, one pitch shift, MP3 encode) of an existing third-party recording, music track or sound-design asset. Nothing was synthesised in code and nothing was produced with an AI audio generator by this project. Machine-readable versions of this page: `public/sfx/credits.json` (per file) and `public/sfx/manifest.json` (ids the app can play).

**2026-11-24 (audio3):** 71 new sound ids (owl calls per species, the moving boxes, furniture by material, the shop till and door bell, gacha shimmer and reveal chimes, book, globe, Pip's hops by surface, birds, frogs, thunder, wind gusts, particle pops, hand-made transition whooshes, wood/paper/brass UI ticks) and 6 new ambience beds (dawn/day forest, dusk thrushes, night peepers, rain on the roof, room tone with a wood stove, snow hush). Short sounds, old and new, are now packed into category **audio sprites** (`sfx_*.mp3`; clip offsets in `manifest.json` under `sprites`, a file entry `sfx_home.mp3#box_tape_2` names a clip), and the loops, music and alarms into three **file packs** (`pack_beds/sleep/music.mp3`: the original MP3 files stored back to back, byte for byte; byte ranges under `packs`, a file entry `pack_beds.mp3#amb_rain` names a member) so the artifact ships 11 files here instead of 140. The license evidence files moved to `docs/audio-licenses/`. Every new sound is cut from a recording by Joseph Sardin (BigSoundBank, CC0), from the project owner's game BUSHED (itself made from CC0 / public-domain recordings, credited below), or from packs already listed here (Kenney, rubberduck). Nothing was synthesised.

## Licenses at a glance

Counted per row of `credits.json` (one row per sprite clip or loop file).

| License | Rows | Attribution required? |
|---|---|---|
| CC0 1.0 Universal | 211 | No. CC0 waives it; several authors say a credit is appreciated |
| From BUSHED (the project owner's game): its files are made only from CC0 1.0, Public Domain Mark 1.0 and U.S. federal (NPS / USFWS) public-domain recordings | 69 | No. Upstream authors are listed per clip in `credits.json` (`upstream`) and in the Sources table |
| Public domain (U.S. National Park Service; credit requested) | 8 | Not a legal condition, but NPS asks: please credit the "National Park Service" where appropriate |
| Public domain (Creative Commons Copyright-Only Dedication / Public Domain Certification 1.0 US) | 1 | No. The creator dedicated the recording to the public domain |

No file uses CC-BY, CC-BY-SA, NC, ND, GPL or a custom "royalty-free" license.

## How licenses were verified

All checks were made on **2026-11-10** (first 88 files) and **2026-11-20** (35 added files) from the build environment, by downloading each source page and each archive and reading them directly. Nothing below relies on a search-result filter or on a third-party summary.

### OpenGameArt.org submissions (CC0)

- For every submission used, the submission page itself was downloaded and its **`License(s)`** field was read. A source was accepted only when that field lists **CC0 and nothing else**, with the label linking to `http://creativecommons.org/publicdomain/zero/1.0/`. Submissions offering several licenses, or CC-BY / OGA-BY / CC-BY-SA / GPL, were not used.
- The exact field found on each page (label and link), the page's own "Copyright/Attribution Notice", the posting date, and sha256 hashes of the page copy and of the downloaded source files are recorded in `docs/audio-licenses/source-license-evidence.json`. The generator of this document refuses to run if any of those pages lists a license other than CC0.
- Where an archive ships its own license file, that file was read too and is copied verbatim into `docs/audio-licenses/`:
  - **Kenney** – *Interface Sounds* `License.txt`: "License: (Creative Commons Zero, CC0) … This content is free to use in personal, educational and commercial projects. Support us by crediting Kenney or www.kenney.nl (this is not mandatory)". *RPG Audio* and *Music Jingles* `license.txt`: "License (CC0) … You may use these graphics in personal and commercial projects. Credit (Kenney or www.kenney.nl) would be nice but is not mandatory."
  - **Vehicle** – *Tinysized SFX* `readme.txt`: "Distributed under Creative Commons Zero".
  - **qubodup** – *Squeaky Rat* `notes.txt`: "released under CC0. The sounds used to create these were under CC0 at the time of creation."
  - **Vehicle** – *Fantasy Accessory SFX Library* `readme.txt` (added 2026-11-20): "Distributed under Creative Commons Zero".
- The Kenney packs were taken from Kenney's own uploads on OpenGameArt (author account "Kenney"), because `kenney.nl` answered HTTP 403 to this environment. They are the same packs, with Kenney's license file inside each zip.

### U.S. National Park Service recordings (public domain)

- Eight recordings come from nps.gov: `amb_wind.mp3`, `owl_hoot.mp3`, `bee_buzz.mp3`, `frog_croak.mp3` (2026-11-10) and `alarm_birds.mp3`, `amb_brown.mp3`, `amb_waves.mp3`, `amb_forest_night.mp3` (2026-11-20). They are **public domain, not CC0**.
- Yellowstone Sound Library page (`https://www.nps.gov/yell/learn/photosmultimedia/soundlibrary.htm`) states: "The files available here were recorded in the park and are in the public domain. They may be downloaded and used without limitation; however, please credit the "National Park Service" where appropriate."
- NPS Natural Sounds – Sound Gallery page (`https://www.nps.gov/subjects/sound/gallery.htm`) states: "The files are in the public domain and may be downloaded. Enjoy, and please give the appropriate credit."
- Each recording's own page was read for its credit line (wind: "NPS/Peter Comley"; owl, bee, pig frog: "NPS"). The statements and page entries are quoted in `docs/audio-licenses/nps_*_public-domain-statement.txt`.
- **Requested credit:** "National Park Service". It is a courtesy request, not a license condition; the suggested credits line above includes it.
- 2026-11-20: `alarm_birds.mp3` (Dawn Chorus, credit "NPS/Jennifer Jerrett") and `amb_brown.mp3` (Hurricane Vent, credit "NPS/Peter Comley") come from the same Yellowstone Sound Library, whose statement was re-read that day (page unchanged).
- 2026-11-20: `amb_waves.mp3` and `amb_forest_night.mp3` come from the NPS multimedia library, found through the NPS audio API (`developer.nps.gov/api/v1/multimedia/audio`). They are not on a library page with its own public-domain statement, so they were accepted only because their media records credit the NPS itself ("NPS, Kristal Ip", San Francisco Maritime NHP; "NSNSD / Emma Brown", Weir Farm NHP, NSNSD being the NPS Natural Sounds and Night Skies Division), and the NPS website disclaimer (`https://www.nps.gov/aboutus/disclaimer.htm`) states: "material created by the National Park Service and presented on this website, unless otherwise indicated, is generally considered in the public domain." The records and the disclaimer are quoted in `docs/audio-licenses/nps_multimedia-library_public-domain-statement.txt`. Recordings in that library credited to outside people (e.g. university or private recordists) were not used.

### Internet Archive recording (public-domain dedication)

- `bowl.mp3` is cut from *Singing Bowl Improvisation* (`https://archive.org/details/SingingBowlImprovisation`, creator "luke jones", added 2008-07-08, description "Short back garden singing bowl improv."). The item's `licenseurl` is `http://creativecommons.org/licenses/publicdomain/`, which resolves to the Creative Commons "Copyright-Only Dedication (based on United States law) or Public Domain Certification 1.0" deed. That is a public-domain dedication, not CC0; Creative Commons retired the tool in 2010 but dedications made with it still apply. The metadata and a deed excerpt are in `docs/audio-licenses/archive-org_singing-bowl-improvisation_public-domain-dedication.txt`.

### 2026-11-24 additions (audio3)

- **BigSoundBank** (`bigsoundbank.com`, Joseph Sardin): every sound page used was downloaded and read; each states "License CC0 (public domain): Free and royalty-free". The site's license page (`https://bigsoundbank.com/licenses.html`) says sounds marked "Free and royalty-free" are "Creative Commons CC0 1.0 Universal". The statement is quoted in `docs/audio-licenses/bigsoundbank-and-bushed.txt`; page and file sha256 hashes are in `source-license-evidence.json` (`audio3` block). Files were taken as FLAC (WAV/MP3 where no FLAC was offered).
- **BUSHED** (`lpx-bushed/games/bushed/public/assets/sfx`, the project owner's game): its `CREDITS.md` says every file there is made by its `tools/sounds/build_sounds.py` from CC0 and public-domain recordings, plus "a little procedural synthesis (numpy noise, tones, thumps)". Its `tools/sounds/recipes.json` shows which events use a synthesised layer (`axe_chop`, `foot_grass`, `foot_forest`, `foot_land`, `foot_porch`, `branch_swish`, `bottle_set`, `amb_store`, `amb_drone` and a few others): **none of those were used**. Only events built purely from recordings were taken; each clip's upstream recordings (title, author, licence, page) are copied into `credits.json` (`upstream`) from BUSHED's recipes, and BUSHED's sound credits are excerpted in `docs/audio-licenses/bigsoundbank-and-bushed.txt`.
- No owl recording in the public domain was found for the snowy owl, Eurasian eagle-owl, great grey owl, burrowing owl, saw-whet owl or little owl (NPS's eastern screech owl file is gone, 404). Those species call with a close relative's real recording, played a little slower or faster (`OWL_CALL` in `src/audio/sound.ts`): snowy -> long-eared owl, eagle-owl -> great horned owl, great grey -> barred/spotted owl (`owl_barred`, the NPS spotted owl), small owls -> the tawny owl's "ke-wick".
- Wikimedia Commons owl recordings were not used: the ones found are xeno-canto uploads under CC BY-SA, or of unclear origin.

### What could not be checked

- `kenney.nl`, `freesound.org` and `cdn.freesound.org` returned HTTP 403 from the sites themselves, so no file was taken from them and no page on them was read.
- Two OpenGameArt submissions say they are derived from Freesound uploads: *Fantozzi's Footsteps* (page text: taken from Fantozzi's Freesound pack 10338 "under CC0") and *Squeaky Rat* (`notes.txt`: based on Freesound sounds that "were under CC0 at the time of creation"). The CC0 grant read here is the one on the OpenGameArt page / in the archive; the original Freesound pages could not be re-checked.
- *UI Sound Effects* by Robin Lamb says its sounds come from the public-domain sample libraries VCSL and VSCO 2 CE; those libraries' own pages were not read. The CC0 grant read is the OpenGameArt one.
- 2026-11-20: `freesound.org` pages loaded (downloads still need a login), so the upstream pages of the two OwlStorm blanket recordings used for `fabric_1/2` were read (Freesound 320144 and 320142: "Creative Commons 0").
- `archive.org` licences are set by the uploader and are not reviewed by the site, the same trust model as OpenGameArt. Only one archive.org item is used (`bowl.mp3`). `commons.wikimedia.org` answered HTTP 429 and was not used.
- `freesound.org`, `kenney.nl` and `pixabay.com` files were not downloaded (login wall / HTTP 403).

### Origin of the sounds

- Nothing in this set was generated with an AI model or synthesised in code by this project.
- The sources are a mix of field/foley recordings (footsteps, rustles, water, crunches, birds, stream, fire, crickets, owl, bee, frog, cat, coins, paper) and human-made sound design with instruments or synthesizers (Kenney's interface tones and jingles, the chimes, the win jingle, the magic and power-up sounds), as described by their authors on the source pages.
- Whether an uploader used generative tools cannot be proven from a web page. Of the 32 OpenGameArt submissions used, 27 were posted between 2010 and 2022. The five newer ones are *Chimey UI Sounds* (2024, "I decided to make my own"), *Kalimba Two-Notes* (2024, "two notes played on a kalimba"), *Various Sound Effects* (2024, "scraps from various old unfinished projects") and *Power-Up Sound Effects* (2024) by Spring Spring, and *Elevator ding* (2025, "made using some synth in Garage Band"). The four NPS files are field recordings.
- 2026-11-20 additions. Field recordings: rain (*AMB Rain Loop 2*, "Recorded on a GoPro Hero 12 Black", 2025) and the four NPS recordings. Acoustic instrument recording: the singing bowl (2008). Foley recordings: clock ticks (2011), clock winding (2020), book pages (2017), pencil, blankets, cloth flip, yawn (OwlishMedia, 2018), stationery thump (2018), drawer ("recorded on my phone", 2022), wood knocks (2018), door creak and cloth flap (Kenney, 2014), glass vials and coins ("All sounds are organic and unprocessed", 2015). Music: *Happy Lullaby* (2013), *Heavenly Loop* ("made using only ZynAddSubFX in LMMS", 2017), *Short kalimba loop* (2017), *A Small Fire Will Do* (2021), *4 Music Box Tracks* (2022, published by the author as MIDI files plus looping OGG renders; tags "music box", "celesta"). Sound design: *Chimey UI Sounds* (2024, "I decided to make my own") for `breath_in`/`breath_out`; the VCSL-derived `Ding.wav` (Robin Lamb) for `breath_hold`.
- Of the 2026-11-20 sources, two OpenGameArt submissions were posted after 2022: *Chimey UI Sounds* (2024) and *AMB Rain Loop 2* (2025, a GoPro field recording). Nothing was generated or synthesised by this project.

## Notes for maintainers

- Sounds were chosen by reading file names and descriptions and by measuring duration, envelope, pitch contour and spectrum. They were **not auditioned by ear**, so a listening pass is worth doing; `manifest.json` gains are suggestions derived from measured loudness.
- One-shots: mono, 44.1 kHz, 112 kbps MP3, leading silence trimmed, peak about -3 dBFS (`ui_type` and `voice_*` about -8 dBFS). Loops: stereo, 44.1 kHz, 128 kbps MP3, about -20 LUFS, 0.5 s fade in and out (the app is expected to crossfade).
- `pet_purr.mp3` is a real cat purr with almost all of its energy below 300 Hz; it is quiet on phone speakers.
- `amb_night.mp3` is an 11.5 s seamless cricket loop repeated three times; `amb_birds.mp3` contains natural pauses between calls.
- Some slots reuse a pack sound under a different role than its file name suggests (for example `ui_error` is Kenney's `question_004`, `plant_dig` is a gravel step, `voice_d` is a short rat squeak). The table above lists every original file.
- 2026-11-20 additions: categories `alarm`, `breath`, `ambient`, `music`, `foley` and `reward` (sparkle). The new soundscapes use `ambient` as requested; the older `amb_*` ids still use `ambience`, so code that filters by category should accept both (or rename one of them).
- Loops (`alarm_*`, new `amb_*`, `music_*`) have `loop: true` and **no fade-in/out**. Soundscapes and `alarm_birds` were made seamless by crossfading the last 1-3 s of the cut into its first 1-3 s (equal-power, ffmpeg `acrossfade`); `alarm_musicbox`, `alarm_kalimba` and `alarm_bells` are cut to whole bars (4 bars) with a 60 ms crossfade; `music_*` are the authors' own loops, kept whole. MP3 adds ~25 ms of encoder delay/padding: Chrome and Firefox remove it using the LAME header written by ffmpeg, Safari may not; if a gap is audible, play the decoded buffer with `loop = true` and `loopEnd` set to the length given in the edits column, or crossfade two players.
- Loudness: soundscapes about -20 LUFS (`amb_forest_night` -22 LUFS; `amb_waves` only reaches -24.7 LUFS under the -3 dBFS peak ceiling because of wave splashes, so its manifest gain is higher, 0.55); music peaks at -6 dBFS (about -19 to -21 LUFS, `music_breath` -18 LUFS); alarms and one-shots peak at -3 dBFS. `breath_hold` is meant to be very soft (gain 0.22).
- `yawn.mp3` is a human yawn (OwlishMedia) raised 4 semitones so it reads as a small creature; the only CC0 creature yawn found (`as_d_yawn.wav` in *NHFEA: Sound*) is low and growly by measurement, so it was not used. `sparkle_1..3` are glass vials clinking (Vehicle), high-passed at 1 kHz. `wings_flutter_1/2` are cloth flaps (Kenney `cloth1`, OwlishMedia `flip`), chosen because they are softer than the existing `bird_flap`. `stamp.mp3` is an unlabelled thump from OwlishMedia's stationery set (`Stationery_17.wav`).
- `music_lullaby` (*cute tune*) and `alarm_musicbox` (*happy polka*) are different tracks from the same music-box pack, so the bedtime tune is never the wake-up tune.
- 2026-11-24 (audio3): short sounds live in audio sprites, mono 44.1 kHz MP3 (80 kbps; `sfx_weather` 64 kbps), decoded whole (at 32 kHz). Loops, music and alarms live in file packs: each member is a complete MP3 (with its own LAME header), fetched with an HTTP range request (or sliced from the whole pack when the host ignores ranges) and decoded on its own. Clips are separated by 0.3 s of silence; the build decodes each sprite again and checks the clip offsets by cross-correlation (no shift: ffmpeg/LAME's encoder delay is trimmed by the Info header, as browsers do). `src/audio/sprites.test.ts` checks every clip against the encoded length, every id the code asks for, and that every clip has a credit row.
- 2026-11-24: older one-shots were decoded from their MP3s and re-encoded into the sprites (one more generation of MP3; gains unchanged); older loops, music and alarms are unchanged, byte for byte, inside the packs. The new ambience beds are 40 s stereo MP3s at 64 kbps (rain on the roof 80 kbps), about -24 to -27 LUFS, 0.5 s fades; the app crossfades them. `bed_rain`, `bed_wind`, `bed_stream` and `bed_fire` are manifest aliases of `amb_rain/amb_wind/amb_stream/amb_fire` so the day ambience and the night soundscape keep separate loops of the same file.
- 2026-11-24: new sounds were again chosen by measuring envelopes, pitch and spectrum, not by ear. Worth a listening pass: `owl_barn` (a real barn owl screech, low-passed at 3.8 kHz and kept quiet), `amb_snow` (quiet forest wind raised 18 dB and low-passed), `wind_gust_3` (raised 26 dB).
